Plan: reduce default group size to 67% of FJ\\n\\nDefault lives in one place on each side: `default_questions_per_group_for_n` (`src/utils/sort_compare.py:293`) and `defaultQuestionsPerGroupForN` (`client/utils/sortCompare.ts:97`). Both currently clamp **FJ(n)** itself. Range is `question_budget_bounds` / `questionBudgetBounds`: `[ceil(FJ/2), FJ×2]` capped at unique pairs — leave those unchanged. Top-n vs full-sort already share this helper (no ranking-mode branch). Configure, Reset, item add/remove, and vote budgets all go through it when `*_explicit` is false.\\n\\n### 1. Implementation steps\\n\\n1. **Scale then clamp (Python + TS, keep in lockstep)**  \\n   For `n >= 2`: `scaled = round(FJ(n) * 2/3)`, then `clamp_questions_per_group(scaled, n)` / `clampQuestionsPerGroup(...)`. Apply 67% *before* clamp so the existing floor wins when scaled is too small (e.g. n=2: 0.67→1 after clamp). Leave `n < 2` fallback (`COMPARE_DEFAULT_QUESTIONS_PER_GROUP` / `DEFAULT_QUESTIONS_PER_GROUP = 20`) alone. Use `2/3` (not `0.67`) and the same rounding in both languages (`Math.round` / equivalent half-up) so Python and TS never drift.\\n\\n2. **Do not touch range, FJ, confidence, or explicit saves**  \\n   No changes to `ford_johnson_budget`, `question_budget_bounds`, clamp helpers, ranking confidence, pair scheduler, or `*_explicit == true` paths. Slider/input `:min`/`:max` stay on bounds. Hint can still show the full FJ **estimate**; only the prefilled value moves.\\n\\n3. **Inherit via the existing default helper (no migration)**  \\n   Non-explicit projects already recompute from this helper at configure, Reset, item sync (`sync_project_group_sizes`), and vote (`resolved_question_budgets`). Changing the helper is enough. Prefer Pydantic models over raw dictionaries\n24: - Structure: exported router, sub-routes, utilities, static content, types (models, schemas)\n25: - Omit curly braces for single-line conditionals\n26: - Write concise one-line conditional syntax\n27: \n28: ## Error Handling\n29: \n30: - Handle edge cases at function entry points\n31: - Employ early returns for error conditions\n32: - Place happy path logic last\n33: - Avoid unnecessary else statements; use if-return patterns\n34: - Implement guard clauses for preconditions\n35: - Provide proper error logging and user-friendly messaging\n36: \n37: ## FastAPI-Specific Guidelines\n38: \n39: - Use functional components (plain functions) and Pydantic models for input validation\n40: - Declare routes with clear return type annotations\n41: - Prefer lifespan context managers for managing startup and shutdown events\n42: - Leverage middleware for logging, error monitoring, and optimization\n43: - Use HTTPException for expected errors and model them as specific HTTP responses\n44: - Apply Pydantic's BaseModel consistently for validation\n45: \n46: ## Performance Optimization\n47: \n48: - Minimize blocking I/O; use async for all database and API calls\n49: - Implement caching with Redis or in-memory stores\n50: - Optimize Pydantic serialization/deserialization\n\n(Showing lines 1-50 of 61. Use offset=51 to continue.)\n</content>","metadata":{"preview":"---\nname: fastapi-python\ndescription: Expert in FastAPI Python development with best practices for APIs and async operations\n---\n\n# FastAPI Python\n\nYou are an expert in FastAPI and Python backend development.\n\n## Key Principles\n\n- Write concise, technical responses with accurate Python examples\n- Favor functional, declarative programming over class-based approaches\n- Prioritize modularization to eliminate code duplication\n- Use descriptive variable names with auxiliary verbs (e.g., `is_active`, `has_permission`)\n- Employ lowercase with underscores for file/directory naming (e.g., `routers/user_routes.py`)\n- Export routes and utilities explicitly\n- Follow the RORO (Receive an Object, Return an Object) pattern\n\n## Python/FastAPI Standards","truncated":true,"loaded":[],"display":{"type":"file","path":"/workspaces/powerchoice/.agents/skills/fastapi-python/SKILL.md","text":"---\nname: fastapi-python\ndescription: Expert in FastAPI Python development with best practices for APIs and async operations\n---\n\n# FastAPI Python\n\nYou are an expert in FastAPI and Python backend development.\n\n## Key Principles\n\n- Write concise, technical responses with accurate Python examples\n- Favor functional, declarative programming over class-based approaches\n- Prioritize modularization to eliminate code duplication\n- Use descriptive variable names with auxiliary verbs (e.g., `is_active`, `has_permission`)\n- Employ lowercase with underscores for file/directory naming (e.g., `routers/user_routes.py`)\n- Export routes and utilities explicitly\n- Follow the RORO (Receive an Object, Return an Object) pattern\n\n## Python/FastAPI Standards\n\n- Use `def` for pure functions, `async def` for asynchronous operations\n- Use type hints for all function signatures. Prefer Pydantic models over raw dictionaries\n- Structure: exported router, sub-routes, utilities, static content, types (models, schemas)\n- Omit curly braces for single-line conditionals\n- Write concise one-line conditional syntax\n\n## Error Handling\n\n- Handle edge cases at function entry points\n- Employ early returns for error conditions\n- Place happy path logic last\n- Avoid unnecessary else statements; use if-return patterns\n- Implement guard clauses for preconditions\n- Provide proper error logging and user-friendly messaging\n\n## FastAPI-Specific Guidelines\n\n- Use functional components (plain functions) and Pydantic models for input validation\n- Declare routes with clear return type annotations\n- Prefer lifespan context managers for managing startup and shutdown events\n- Leverage middleware for logging, error monitoring, and optimization\n- Use HTTPException for expected errors and model them as specific HTTP responses\n- Apply Pydantic's BaseModel consistently for validation\n\n## Performance Optimization\n\n- Minimize blocking I/O; use async for all database and API calls\n- Implement caching with Redis or in-memory stores\n- Optimize Pydantic serialization/deserialization","lineStart":1,"lineEnd":50,"totalLines":61,"truncated":true}},"title":".agents/skills/fastapi-python/SKILL.md","time":{"start":1787314384629,"end":1787314386437}},"id":"prt_0243d2e76001y7A0EXHSHfROQY","sessionID":"ses_fdbc30787ffefNia54uWevQPBe","messageID":"msg_0243d080c001k87yyhHwDcFO5d"}}
